of
12
.)

Lemma 14
.

Let
({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})
be
well-formed
.
If
({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}{m}}}}$}}\,

and
({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}^{\prime}{m}^{\prime}}}}$}}\,

then
\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}=\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}^{\prime}
.

Proof A.14
.

We proceed by induction on the derivation of the transition
({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}{m}}}}$}}\,
, analysing the last rule applied of those in
Equation

2.4
.
We only show the case of rule
\left[{\mathtt{{act}}}\right]
, as the only other applicable case
(rule
\left[{\mathtt{{unfold}}}\right]
) follows by induction hypothesis as in
12
and
A.5
of
12
.
Therefore, if rule
\left[{\mathtt{{act}}}\right]
, then
{S}=\begin{array}[t]{\{}{@{}l@{}}\}{\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{i}}\,{{l}_{i}}\left\langle{{T}_{i}}\right\rangle{\left({{{\delta}_{i}},{{\lambda}_{i}}}\right)}.{{S}_{i}}\end{array}_{i\in I}
and:

({{\nu},}{\penalty\ \begin{array}[t]{\{}{@{}l@{}}\}{\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{i}}\,{{l}_{i}}\left\langle{{T}_{i}}\right\rangle{\left({{{\delta}_{i}},{{\lambda}_{i}}}\right)}.{{S}_{i}}\end{array}_{i\in I}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{j}{m}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}\left[{{{\lambda}_{j}}\mapsto 0}\right],}{\penalty\ {S}_{j}}){\nu}\models\delta_{j}\quad{m}={{{l}}_{j}\left\langle{{T}}_{j}\right\rangle}\quad j\in I

(A.3)

where
\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}=\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{j}
and
{m}={{l}}_{j}\left\langle{{T}}_{j}\right\rangle
.
Since
({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})
is
well-formed
, by
Section

2.3.1
,
\exists{\delta}
such that
{\nu}\models{\delta}
and
\varnothing;{\delta}\Entails\begin{array}[t]{\{}{@{}l@{}}\}{\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{i}}\,{{l}_{i}}\left\langle{{T}_{i}}\right\rangle{\left({{{\delta}_{i}},{{\lambda}_{i}}}\right)}.{{S}_{i}}\end{array}_{i\in I}
.
The only possible rule is
\left[{\mathtt{{choice}}}\right]
:

{A};\penalty\ \downarrow{\bigvee\limits_{i\in I}{\delta}_{i}}\Entails\begin{array}[t]{\{}{@{}l@{}}\}{\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{i}}\,{{l}_{i}}\left\langle{{T}_{i}}\right\rangle{\left({{{\delta}_{i}},{{\lambda}_{i}}}\right)}.{{S}_{i}}\end{array}_{i\in I}\begin{array}[]{@{}c @{\hspace{2ex}} l@{}}\forall i\in I:{A};\penalty\ {\gamma}_{i}\Entails{S}_{i}\penalty\ \land\penalty\ {\delta}_{i}\left[{{{\lambda}_{i}}\mapsto 0}\right]\models{\gamma}_{i}\hfil\hskip 8.61108pt&\text{(feasibility)}\\[2.15277pt]
\forall i,j\in I:i\neq j\implies{\delta}_{i}\land{\delta}_{j}\models\MathOpFalse\penalty\ \vee\penalty\ \mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{i}=\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{j}\hfil\hskip 8.61108pt&\text{(mixed-choice)}\\[2.15277pt]
\forall i\in I:{T}_{i}=\begin{array}[c]{(}{@{}l@{}}){\delta}^{\prime}{}^{\prime},\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}\end{array}\implies\varnothing;\penalty\ {\gamma}^{\prime}\Entails{S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}\penalty\ \land\penalty\ {\delta}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}\models{\gamma}^{\prime}\hfil\hskip 8.61108pt&\text{(delegation)}\end{array}

As in
10
, it follows that
{\delta}_{j}\models\downarrow{\bigvee_{i\in I}{\delta}_{i}}
.
Since
({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}^{\prime}{m}^{\prime}}}}$}}\,
, we proceed by induction on the derivation of the transition, analysing the last rule applied. Again, we omit the case by rule
\left[{\mathtt{{unfold}}}\right]
.
By rule
\left[{\mathtt{{act}}}\right]
, it follows similarly to
Equation

A.3
: for some
k\in I
,
\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}^{\prime}=\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{k}
,
{m}={{l}}_{k}\left\langle{{T}}_{k}\right\rangle
and
{\nu}\models{\delta}_{k}
.
In the case where
j=k
, then the thesis coincides with the hypothesis.
Otherwise, if
j\neq k
, then by the
(mixed-choice)
premise of rule
\left[{\mathtt{{choice}}}\right]
,

{\delta}_{j}\land{\delta}_{k}\models\MathOpFalse
or
\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{j}=\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{k}
.
Since
{\nu}\models{\delta}_{j}
and
{\nu}\models{\delta}_{k}
, it follows that
{\delta}_{j}\land{\delta}_{k}\not\models\MathOpFalse
.
Therefore, it must be that
\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{j}=\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{k}
and we obtain our thesis.

A.6.
System Transition Preservations

Lemma 15
.

Let
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})
and
({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2}})
be
well-formed
,
and
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\Compat({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})
.
If
{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}\mid{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}\ {\rightarrow}\ {({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}})}
,
then
({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1}})
and
({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2}})
are
well-formed
.

Proof A.15
.

We proceed by cases on the rule used in the derivation of the transition:

{({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})}\ {\rightarrow}\ {({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}})}

analysing the last rule applied
of those given in
Equation

2.6
:

Case 1.

If rule
\left[{\mathtt{{wait}}}\right]
, then:

{({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}}}}$}}\,{({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}})}{({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}}}}$}}\,{({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})}&{({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}}}}$}}\,{({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}})}

Hereafter, we only show the case for
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})
, as the transition for
({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})
is analogous.
We proceed by inner induction on the derivation of the transition

{({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}}}}$}}\,{({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})}

analysing the last rule applied.
By rule
\left[{\mathtt{{time}}}\right]
:

({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\begin{array}[t]{@{}c @{\quad} l@{}}({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1}})&\text{(configuration)}\\[2.15277pt]
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})\penalty\ \text{is $\mathtt{fe}$}\implies({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1}})\penalty\ \text{is $\mathtt{fe}$}&\text{(persistency)}\\[0.0pt]
\forall{t}^{\prime}<{t}:({{\nu}_{1}+{t}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\,{{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{\tau}}}$}}}\,&\text{(urgency)}\end{array}

By inner induction on the derivation of the transition

({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1}})

analysing the last rule applied of those in
Equation

2.4
:

i.

If rule
\left[{\mathtt{{tick}}}\right]
,
then by
12
of
12
,
{\nu}^{\prime}_{1}={\nu}_{1}+{t}
,
\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}
and
{S}^{\prime}_{1}\UTU{S}_{1}
.
If
{t}=0
then
{\nu}_{1}={\nu}_{1}+{t}
and therefore, the thesis coincides with the hypothesis.
By the
(urgency)
premise of rule
\left[{\mathtt{{time}}}\right]
,
{t}
must be valued such that no message can be received from the queue
\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}
via rule
\left[{\mathtt{{recv}}}\right]
.
If
{t}=0
, then
\nexists{t}^{\prime}<{t}
, and the
(urgency)
premise of rule
\left[{\mathtt{{time}}}\right]
always holds, even if
({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{\tau}}}$}}\,
.
However, since
{\nu}^{\prime}_{1}={\nu}_{1}
, this is the same as no transition occurring via rule
\left[{\mathtt{{time}}}\right]
.
Otherwise,
{t}>0
.
Since
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})
is
well-formed
, it follows
18
,
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})
is also
future-enabled
.
Since
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})
is
future-enabled
, it follows the
(persistency)
premise of rule
\left[{\mathtt{{time}}}\right]
that
({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1}})
is also
future-enabled
.
By
10
,
({{\nu}_{1}+{t},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1}})
is
well-formed
.

ii.

If rule
\left[{\mathtt{{unfold}}}\right]
,
then
{S}_{1}=\mu\alpha.{S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}
and:

({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mu\alpha.{S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{\ell}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1}})({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}\left[{\nicefrac{{\mu\alpha.{S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}}}{{\alpha}}}\right]})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{\ell}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1}})

where
\ell={t}
.
Since
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mu\alpha.{S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}})
is
well-formed
,

\exists{\delta}
such that
{\nu}_{1}\models{\delta}
and
\varnothing;{\delta}\Entails\mu\alpha.{S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}
.
By rule
\left[{\mathtt{{rec}}}\right]
:

\varnothing;{\delta}\Entails\mu\alpha.{S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}\alpha:{\delta};{\delta}\Entails{S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}\left[{\nicefrac{{\mu\alpha.{S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}}}{{\alpha}}}\right]

By
2
:

\alpha:{\delta};{\delta}\Entails{S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}\left[{\nicefrac{{\mu\alpha.{S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}}}{{\alpha}}}\right]\iff\varnothing;{\delta}\Entails{S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}
.
By
Section

A.1
,
it follows

\mu\alpha.{S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}\UTU{S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}\left[{\nicefrac{{\mu\alpha.{S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}}}{{\alpha}}}\right]
.
By
5
, since

\varnothing;{\delta}\Entails{S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}
,
then

\varnothing;{\delta}\Entails\mu\alpha.{S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}
.
By
6
,
if
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}\left[{\nicefrac{{\mu\alpha.{S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}}}{{\alpha}}}\right]})
is
well-formed
,
then
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mu\alpha.{S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}})
is
well-formed
.
The thesis holds by the induction hypothesis. (As in
2
in
10
.)

Case 2.

\left[{\mathtt{{com}}\text{-}{\mathtt{l}}}\right]
, then:

{({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{\tau}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}})({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{!{m}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})&({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{?{m}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}})

First, we proceed by cases on the derivation of the transition

\left[{\mathtt{{que}}}\right]
:

Lemma 16
.

Let
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})
and
({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2}})
be
well-formed
, and
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\Compat({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})
.
If
{({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})}\ \,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}}}}$}}\,\ {({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}})}

and
{t}>0
,
then
\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}=\varnothing=\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}
.

Proof A.16
.

We proceed by cases on the derivation of the transition

({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}}}}$}}\,

analysing the last rule applied of those in
Equation

2.6
.
By rule
\left[{\mathtt{{wait}}}\right]
:

{({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}}}}$}}\,{({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}})}{({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})&{({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}})

where, as in
Section

A.5
of
13
,
\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}
and
\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}
.
Hereafter, we only show
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})
as
({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})
is analogous.
By cases on the derivation of the transition

{({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})

analysing the last rule applied.
By rule
\left[{\mathtt{{time}}}\right]
:

({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\begin{array}[t]{@{}c @{\quad} l@{}}({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1}})&\text{(configuration)}\\[-8.61108pt]
\\
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})\penalty\ \text{is $\mathtt{fe}$}\implies({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1}})\penalty\ \text{is $\mathtt{fe}$}&\text{(persistency)}\\[-9.68747pt]
\\
\hphantom{\forall{t}^{\prime}<{t}:({{\nu}_{1}+{t}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\,{{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{\tau}}}$}}}\,}\mathllap{\smash{\forall{t}^{\prime}<{t}:({{\nu}_{1}+{t}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\,{{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{\tau}}}$}}}\,}}&\text{(urgency)}\end{array}

where, as in
Section

A.5
of
13
,
{\nu}^{\prime}_{1}={\nu}_{1}+{t}
and
{S}^{\prime}_{1}\UTU{S}_{1}
.
Suppose by contradiction that one of the queues
\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}
were
non-empty
, and
\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}={m};\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}
.
Since
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ {m};\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\Compat({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})
, by
Item

2
in
Section

2.3.3
:

\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}={m};\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}\implies({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{?{m}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1}})\land({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})\Compat({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})

Such a transition is only viable by rule
\left[{\mathtt{{recv}}}\right]
.
By
Section

A.5
of
13
, it follows
that
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{?{m}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1}})
is made by the premise of rule
\left[{\mathtt{{recv}}}\right]
.
However, by the
(urgency)
premise of rule
\left[{\mathtt{{time}}}\right]
,
\forall{t}^{\prime}<{t}
no transitions by rule
\left[{\mathtt{{recv}}}\right]
are viable
– which contradicts with
Item

2
in
Section

2.3.3
.
Therefore, it cannot be that either queue is
non-empty
for a transition
{t}
, where
{t}>0
.
For such a transition, both queues must be
empty
.

Lemma 17
.

Let
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})
and
({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2}})
be
well-formed
,
and
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\Compat({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})
.
If
{({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})}\ {\rightarrow}\ {({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}})}
,
then
({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})\Compat({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}})
.

Proof A.17
.

We proceed by cases on the derivation of the transition:

{({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})}\ {\rightarrow}\ {({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}})}

analysing the last rule applied
of those given in
Equation

2.6
:

Case 1.

If rule
\left[{\mathtt{{wait}}}\right]
, then:

{({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}}}}$}}\,{({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}})}({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})\quad({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}})

By
Section

A.5
of
13
, it follows that
{\nu}^{\prime}_{1}={\nu}_{1}+{t}
,
{S}^{\prime}_{1}\UTU{S}_{1}
and
\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}
(and similarly for
{\nu}^{\prime}_{2}
,
{S}^{\prime}_{2}
and
\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}
).
If
{t}=0
then
{\nu}_{1}={\nu}_{1}+{t}
(and
{\nu}_{2}={\nu}_{2}+{t}
) and therefore, the thesis coincides with the hypothesis.
Else
{t}>0
, and by

\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}=\varnothing=\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}
.
By

4
of

2.3.3
:

\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}=\varnothing=\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}\implies{S}_{1}=\overline{{S}_{2}\mathstrut}\penalty\ \land\penalty\ {\nu}_{1}={\nu}_{2}
.
Since
{\nu}_{1}+{t}={\nu}_{2}+{t}
, it follows that
({{\nu}_{1}+{t},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\Compat({{\nu}_{2}+{t},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})
.

First, by induction on the derivation of the transition

({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{?{m}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}})

A.7.
System Progress

Lemma 18
.

Let
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})
and
({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2}})
be
well-formed
.
If
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\Compat({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})
,
then
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})
and
({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})
are
final
,
or
\exists{t}:{({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})}\ \,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}}\penalty\ {\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{\tau}}}}$}}\,
.

Proof A.18
.

Since both
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})
and
({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2}})
are
well-formed
,
by
8
, both
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})
and
({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2}})
are
live
.
By
Section

2.3.2
,
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})
is
final
if
{S}_{1}\UTU\mathtt{end}

(and similarly for
{S}_{2}
).
By
Section

A.1
if
{S}_{1}\NUTU\mathtt{end}
, then
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})
is
future-enabled
(and similarly for
({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2}})
).
By
Section

2.2.1
,
\exists{t}^{\prime}
such that
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}^{\prime}}\penalty\ {\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}{m}}}}}$}}\,

(and similarly for
({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2}})
).
Therefore, we have obtained our thesis as it holds that if,
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})
is
not
final the by
Section

A.1
, it
must
be
future-enabled
by
Section

2.2.1
, which coincides with the hypothesis.

Lemma 19
.

If
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})
and
({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2}})
are
well-formed
,
and
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\Compat({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})

and
{({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})}\,\longrightarrow^{*}\,{({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}})}
,
then
{({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})\Compat({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}})}
and
({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1}})
and
({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2}})
are
well-formed
.

Proof A.19
.

We proceed by induction on the length of (
\,\longrightarrow^{*}\,
).
The base case is trivial, as

{({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})}\,\longrightarrow^{0}\,{({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})}

and hence the thesis coincides with the hypothesis.
For the induction case, suppose:

{({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})}\,\longrightarrow^{n}\,{({{\nu}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{2}})}\,\longrightarrow^{\mbox{}}\,{({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})\mid({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}})}

By the induction hypothesis:

({{\nu}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}})\Compat({{\nu}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{2}})
,
and both
({{\nu}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{1}})
and
({{\nu}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}_{2}})
are
well-formed
.
It remains for us to show that
both
({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1}})
and
({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2}})
are
well-formed
,
and
({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}})\Compat({{\nu}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{2}})
.
By
15
and
17
, we obtain our thesis.

Lemma 20
.

The following holds:

\forall{\nu},{S}:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})\text{\ is\ \emph{well-formed}}\implies{({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S},}{\penalty\ \varnothing})\mid({{\nu},}{\penalty\ \overline{{S}\mathstrut},}{\penalty\ \varnothing})}\text{\ satisfies progress.}

Proof A.20
.

Since
({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})
is
well-formed
, by
Section

2.3

({{\nu},}{\penalty\ \overline{{S}\mathstrut}})
is
well-formed
.
By
Section

2.3.3
,
{({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S},}{\penalty\ \varnothing})\Compat({{\nu},}{\penalty\ \overline{{S}\mathstrut},}{\penalty\ \varnothing})}
.
If
{S}=\mathtt{end}
then
\overline{{S}\mathstrut}=\mathtt{end}
and therefore, by
Section

2.3.2
, the thesis coincides with the hypothesis.
Otherwise,
{({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S},}{\penalty\ \varnothing})\mid({{\nu},}{\penalty\ \overline{{S}\mathstrut},}{\penalty\ \varnothing})}

satisfies progress
if, for all
{\mathbf{S}}^{\prime}\mid{\mathbf{S}}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}
reachable from
{({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S},}{\penalty\ \varnothing})\mid({{\nu},}{\penalty\ \overline{{S}\mathstrut},}{\penalty\ \varnothing})}

then,
either
{\mathbf{S}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathbf{S}}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}
are both
final
, or

\exists{t}
such that:

{\mathbf{S}}^{\prime}\mid{\mathbf{S}}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{{t}}\penalty\ {\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{\tau}}}}$}}\,
.
By
19
, any transition made by
{({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S},}{\penalty\ \varnothing})\mid({{\nu},}{\penalty\ \overline{{S}\mathstrut},}{\penalty\ \varnothing})}
will preserve both
well-formedness
and
compatible
.
By
18
, we obtain our thesis.

See
2

Proof A.21
.

The thesis follows immediately from
20
.

As typical for binary systems, our proof of progress builds upon a notion of
duality
between participants.
Since communication is asynchronous, each party may break duality with their co-party.
Compatibility allows the duality of participants to be broken, only if, doing so does not violate
communication safety
.
More specifically, compatibility requires that any message received by a queue is expected (i.e., able to be received), and that the resulting configurations are still
compatible
.
In practice, using compatibility allows each party to behave and progress independently, regardless the state of the other party, while retaining the essence of
duality
, and guaranteeing that all messages will eventually be received.

Appendix B
Proof of Subject Reduction

B.1.
Auxiliary Definitions, Figures & Assumptions

[Free-queues of a Process]

The function
\mathtt{fq}\left({{P}}\right)
returns the set of
free-queues
in a given process
{P}

\mathtt{fq}\left({p^{\diamond{n}}\,{\MathOpPrcRecv\mathstrut}\begin{array}[t]{\{}{@{}l@{}}\}{{l}_{i}}{\left({{v}_{i}}\right)}:{P}_{i}\end{array}_{i\in I}}\right)\cup\mathtt{fq}\left({{Q}}\right)&\text{if\ }{P}=p^{\diamond{n}}\,{\MathOpPrcRecv\mathstrut}\begin{array}[t]{\{}{@{}l@{}}\}{{l}_{i}}{\left({{v}_{i}}\right)}:{P}_{i}\end{array}_{i\in I}\penalty\ \mathtt{after}\penalty\ {{Q}}\\[4.73611pt]
\varnothing&\text{if\ }{P}=\texttt{0},\penalty\ X\langle{\vec{v};\vec{r}}\rangle\end{cases}\end{array}

B.1.1.
Session Environments

[Live
{\Delta}
]

{\Delta}
is
live
if,
for all
p\in\text{dom}({{\Delta}})

such that
{\Delta}={\Delta}^{\prime},{p:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})}\implies

({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})
is
live
by
Section

A.1
.

[Delayable
{\Delta}
]
A session environment
{\Delta}
is
delayable
if:

\forall{qp}\in\text{dom}({{\Delta}}):{\Delta}({qp})\neq\varnothing\implies p\notin\text{dom}({{\Delta}})

B.1.2.
Session Environment Reduction

See
Figure

7
.

\begin{array}[t]{@{}c@{}}\begin{array}[t]{c @{\quad} c}{\Delta}_{1},\penalty\ {\Delta}_{2}\,\longrightarrow^{\mbox{}}\,{\Delta}^{\prime}_{1},\penalty\ {\Delta}_{2}{\Delta}_{1}\,\longrightarrow^{\mbox{}}\,{\Delta}^{\prime}_{1}&{p:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})},\penalty\ {{qp}:{{{l}}\left\langle{{T}}\right\rangle;\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}}}\,\longrightarrow^{\mbox{}}\,{p:({{\nu}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}})},\penalty\ {{qp}:\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}}({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S},}{\penalty\ {{l}}\left\langle{{T}}\right\rangle;\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{\tau}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}})\end{array}\\[-2.15277pt]
\\
\begin{array}[t]{c @{\quad} c}{\Delta}_{1},\penalty\ {\Delta}_{2}\,\longrightarrow^{\mbox{}}\,{\Delta}_{1},\penalty\ {\Delta}^{\prime}_{2}{\Delta}_{2}\,\longrightarrow^{\mbox{}}\,{\Delta}^{\prime}_{2}&{p:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})},\penalty\ {{pq}:\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}},\,\longrightarrow^{\mbox{}}\,{p:({{\nu}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}})},\penalty\ {{pq}:\mathbf{\mathtt{M}};{m}}({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{!{m}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}})\end{array}\end{array}

Figure 7
.
Reduction for Session Environments

B.2.
Delayable Processes

Lemma 1
.

If
\Phi_{t}\left({{P}}\right)
is defined,
then
\mathtt{Wait}\left({{P}}\right)\cap\mathtt{NEQ}\left({{P}}\right)=\varnothing
.

Proof B.1
.

The hypothesis holds by the definition of
\Phi_{t}\left({{P}}\right)
in
Section

3.3
.

B.3.
Well-formed
{\Delta}

Lemma 2
.

Let
{\Delta}
be
well-formed
.
If
{\Delta}
is
balanced
and
{\Delta}\,\longrightarrow^{\mbox{}}\,{\Delta}^{\prime}
,
then
{\Delta}^{\prime}
is
balanced
.
Furthermore, if
{\Delta}
is
fully-balanced
, then
{\Delta}^{\prime}
is
fully-balanced
.

Proof B.2
.

We proceed by induction on the derivation of the reduction
{\Delta}\,\longrightarrow^{\mbox{}}\,{\Delta}^{\prime}
, analysing the last rule applied of those given in
Figure

7
:

Case 1.

If rule
\left[{{{\Delta}}\text{-}\mathtt{{Send}}}\right]
, then
{\Delta}={p:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})},{{pq}:\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}}
and:

{p:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})},{{pq}:\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}}\,\longrightarrow^{\mbox{}}\,{p:({{\nu}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}})},{{pq}:\mathbf{\mathtt{M}};{{l}}\left\langle{{T}}\right\rangle}({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{!{{l}}\left\langle{{T}}\right\rangle}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}})

We have that
{p:({{\nu}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}})},{{pq}:\mathbf{\mathtt{M}};{{l}}\left\langle{{T}}\right\rangle}
is
balanced
, as every condition of
Section

6
holds trivially. Regarding the furthermore part, it holds
trivially as
{\Delta}
is not
fully-balanced
.

Case 2.

If rule
\left[{{{\Delta}}\text{-}\mathtt{{Recv}}}\right]
,
then
{\Delta}={p:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})},{{qp}:{{l}}\left\langle{{T}}\right\rangle;\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}}
and:

{p:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})},{{qp}:{{l}}\left\langle{{T}}\right\rangle;\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}}\,\longrightarrow^{\mbox{}}\,{p:({{\nu}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}})},{{qp}:\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}}({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S},}{\penalty\ {{l}}\left\langle{{T}}\right\rangle;\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{?{{l}}\left\langle{{T}}\right\rangle}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}})

By rule
\left[{\mathtt{{recv}}}\right]
of
Equation

2.5
:

({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S},}{\penalty\ {m};\,\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{\tau}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}})({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{?{m}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}})

By the premise of the above,
thanks to
Item

1
of
Section

6
,
it follows that
({{\nu}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}})
is
balanced
, as required.
The furthermore case holds trivially.

Case 3.

If rule
\left[{{{\Delta}}\text{-}\mathtt{{L}}}\right]
then

{\Delta}={\Delta}_{1},\penalty\ {\Delta}_{2}
and

{\Delta}^{\prime}={\Delta}^{\prime}_{1},\penalty\ {\Delta}_{2}
and:

{\Delta}_{1},\penalty\ {\Delta}_{2}\,\longrightarrow^{\mbox{}}\,{\Delta}^{\prime}_{1},\penalty\ {\Delta}_{2}{\Delta}_{1}\,\longrightarrow^{\mbox{}}\,{\Delta}^{\prime}_{1}

It is easy to see that
{\Delta}_{1}
and
{\Delta}_{2}
are both balanced. By the induction hypothesis also
{\Delta}^{\prime}_{1}
is balanced. Notice that the
composition of balanced environments is not necessarily balanced. So we need to show
that all the items in
Section

6
hold for
{\Delta}^{\prime}_{1}
.
We show only for
Item

1
of
Section

6
, as the other cases are similar.
So, suppose:

{\Delta}^{\prime}={\Delta}^{\prime}{}^{\prime},{p:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})},{{qp}:{{m};\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}}}
,
then we have to show:

({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{?{m}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}})
and
{\Delta}^{\prime},{p:({{\nu}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}})},{{qp}:\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}}\in\text{Bal}
.
The interesting cases are:

•

{\Delta}^{\prime}_{1}(p)=({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})
,
{\Delta}_{2}({qp})={m};\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}
and

{\Delta}_{1}(p)\neq{\Delta}^{\prime}_{1}(p)
. We argue that this case is
impossible. So suppose, by contradiction, it is not the case. Then,
since queue
{qp}
did not change during the transition, it must be

{\Delta}_{1}(p)=(\nu_{0},{S}_{0})
for some
\nu_{0},{S}_{0}
such that:

(\nu_{0},{S}_{0})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{!{m}^{\prime}}}}$}}\,({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})

and
{\Delta}_{1}({pq})=\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}
and
{\Delta}^{\prime}_{1}({pq})=\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime};{m}^{\prime}
.
By
14
,
(\nu_{0},{S}_{0})\,{{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{?{m}}}}$}}}\,

and hence
{\Delta}
was not balanced in the first place: contradiction.

•

{\Delta}_{2}(p)=({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})
,
{\Delta}^{\prime}_{1}({qp})={m};\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}
and

{\Delta}_{1}({qp})\neq{\Delta}^{\prime}_{1}({qp})
. Then
\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}=\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime};{m}^{\prime}
and
{\Delta}_{1}(q)=(\nu_{0},{S}_{0})
,
{\Delta}^{\prime}_{1}(q)=(\nu_{0}^{\prime},{S}^{\prime}_{0})
and

{\Delta}_{1}({qp})={m};\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}
with:

(\nu_{0},{S}_{0})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{!{m}^{\prime}}}}$}}\,(\nu_{0}^{\prime},{S}^{\prime}_{0})
.
By
Item

2
of
Section

6
, for some
\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{0}
:

(\nu,{S},\mathbf{\mathtt{M}})\Compat(\nu_{0},{S}_{0},\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{0})
.
by rule
\left[{\mathtt{{com}}\text{-}{\mathtt{r}}}\right]
in
Equation

2.6
:

{(\nu,{S},{m};\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime})\mid(\nu_{0},{S}_{0},\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{0})}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{\tau}}}$}}\,{(\nu,{S},{m};\mathbf{\mathtt{M}})\mid(\nu^{\prime}_{0},{S}^{\prime}_{0},\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{0})}

By
17
:

(\nu,{S},{m};\mathbf{\mathtt{M}})\Compat(\nu_{0},{S}_{0},\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{0})

Hence
(\nu,{S})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{?{m}}}}$}}\,(\nu^{\prime},{S}^{\prime})
for some
(\nu^{\prime},{S}^{\prime})
such
that
(\nu^{\prime},{S}^{\prime},\mathbf{\mathtt{M}})\Compat(\nu^{\prime}_{0},{S}^{\prime}_{0},\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{0})
.
It remains to show that
{\Delta}^{\prime},{p:({{\nu}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}})},{{qp}:\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}}
is
balanced
by
Section

6
.
For
Items

2
and
3
of
Section

6
it surely holds.
Regarding
Item

1
of
Section

6
, it follows by a simple induction on the length of
\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}
.

B.4.1.
Well-typed Enabled Actions

Lemma 4
.

Let
{\Delta},{p:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})}
be
well-formed
.
If
{\Gamma};{\theta}\Entails{P}\TypedBy{\Delta},{p:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})}

and
({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{?{m}}}}$}}\,

then
p\in\mathtt{Wait}\left({{P}}\right)
.

Proof B.21
.

By induction on the derivation of the transition

({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{?{m}}}}$}}\,
, analysing the last rule applied of those given
in
Equation

2.4
.
The only applicable rules are
\left[{\mathtt{{act}}}\right]
and
\left[{\mathtt{{unfold}}}\right]
.
As in
14
, we only show rule
\left[{\mathtt{{act}}}\right]
:

({{\nu},}{\penalty\ \begin{array}[t]{\{}{@{}l@{}}\}{\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{i}}\,{{l}_{i}}\left\langle{{T}_{i}}\right\rangle{\left({{{\delta}_{i}},{{\lambda}_{i}}}\right)}.{{S}_{i}}\end{array}_{i\in I}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{j}{m}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}\left[{{{\lambda}_{j}}\mapsto 0}\right],}{\penalty\ {S}_{j}}){\nu}\models\delta_{j}\quad{m}={{{l}}_{j}\left\langle{{T}}_{j}\right\rangle}\quad j\in I

where
{S}=\begin{array}[t]{\{}{@{}l@{}}\}{\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{i}}\,{{l}_{i}}\left\langle{{T}_{i}}\right\rangle{\left({{{\delta}_{i}},{{\lambda}_{i}}}\right)}.{{S}_{i}}\end{array}_{i\in I}
.
By inspecting
Figure

3
for when
p\in\mathtt{Wait}\left({{P}}\right)
, it remains for us to show that
{P}
is structured as follows:

{P}\in\begin{array}[t]{\{}{@{\ }l@{\ \ }l@{\ }}\}p^{{e}}\,{\MathOpPrcRecv\mathstrut}\begin{array}[t]{\{}{@{}l@{}}\}{{l}_{j}}{\left({{v}_{j}}\right)}:{P}_{j}\end{array}_{j\in J},&p^{\diamond{n}}\,{\MathOpPrcRecv\mathstrut}\begin{array}[t]{\{}{@{}l@{}}\}{{l}_{j}}{\left({{v}_{j}}\right)}:{P}_{j}\end{array}_{j\in J}\penalty\ \mathtt{after}\penalty\ {{Q}}\end{array}

We proceed by induction on the derivation of

{\Gamma};{\theta}\Entails{P}\TypedBy{\Delta},{p:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})}
,
analysing the last rule applied of those given in
Figures

5
and
6
:

Case 1.

If rule
\left[{\mathtt{{Branch}}}\right]
,
then
{P}=p^{{e}}\,{\MathOpPrcRecv\mathstrut}\begin{array}[t]{\{}{@{}l@{}}\}{{l}_{j}}{\left({{v}_{j}}\right)}:{P}_{j}\end{array}_{j\in J}
and we obtain our thesis.

Case 2.

If rule
\left[{\mathtt{{Timeout}}}\right]
,
then
{P}=p^{\diamond{n}}\,{\MathOpPrcRecv\mathstrut}\begin{array}[t]{\{}{@{}l@{}}\}{{l}_{j}}{\left({{v}_{j}}\right)}:{P}_{j}\end{array}_{j\in J}\penalty\ \mathtt{after}\penalty\ {{Q}}
.

Case 3.

If rule
\left[{\mathtt{{VRecv}}}\right]
,
then
{P}=p^{{e}}\,{\MathOpPrcRecv\mathstrut}\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}{{l}}{\left({{v}}\right)}:{P}^{\prime}\end{array}
.
(The same holds for rule
\left[{\mathtt{{DRecv}}}\right]
.)

Case 4.

If rule
\left[{\mathtt{{VSend}}}\right]
,
then
{P}=p\,{\MathOpPrcSend\mathstrut}\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}{{l}}\left({v}\right)\end{array}.{{P}^{\prime}}
.
Clearly
p\notin\mathtt{Wait}\left({{P}}\right)
, and it remains for us to prove that this case is not applicable.
By rule
\left[{\mathtt{{VSend}}}\right]
:

{\Gamma};{\theta}\Entails p\,{\MathOpPrcSend\mathstrut}\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}{{l}}\left({v}\right)\end{array}.{{P}^{\prime}}\TypedBy{\Delta},{p:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ \begin{array}[t]{\{}{@{}l@{}}\}{\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{i}}\,{{l}_{i}}\left\langle{{T}_{i}}\right\rangle{\left({{{\delta}_{i}},{{\lambda}_{i}}}\right)}.{{S}_{i}}\end{array}_{i\in I}})}\begin{array}[t]{@{}c@{}}\exists j\in I:({l}={l}_{j})\land({\nu}\models{\delta}_{j})\land({T}_{j}\penalty\ \text{\emph{base-type}})\land({\Gamma}\Entails{v}:{T}_{j})\land\mbox{}\\[3.01385pt]
(\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{j}=!)\land{\Gamma};{\theta}\Entails{{P}}\TypedBy{\Delta},\penalty\ {p:({{\nu}\left[{{{\lambda}_{j}}\mapsto 0}\right],}{\penalty\ {S}_{j}})}\end{array}

By
14
, since
({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})
is
well-formed
, it cannot be that both
({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{?{m}}}}$}}\,
and
({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{!{m}^{\prime}}}}$}}\,
, where
{m}^{\prime}={{l}}\left\langle{{T}}\right\rangle
.
Therefore, this case is not applicable since it cannot be that a
sending
process
{P}
is
well-typed
against
p
, which has an enabled
receiving
action.
(The same holds for rule
\left[{\mathtt{{DSend}}}\right]
.)

B.4.2.

Lemma 5
.

Let
{\Delta}
be

.

{\Gamma};{\theta}\Entails{P}\TypedBy{\Delta}
and

,

.

Proof B.22
.

As standard
[

,

,

]
, with the additions in

3.2.1
.

B.4.3.

Lemma 6

(Substitution)
.

Let
{\Delta}
be

.

6
and

both hold.

{\Gamma};{\theta}\Entails{P}\TypedBy{\Delta},{p:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})}

,
then
{\Gamma};{\theta}\Entails{{P}\left[{\nicefrac{{b}}{{p}}}\right]}\TypedBy{\Delta},{b:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})}
.

If
{\Gamma}_{1},{{m}:{T}};{\theta}\Entails{P}\TypedBy{\Delta}

and
{\Gamma}_{2}\Entails{{v}:{T}}

and
\text{dom}({{\Gamma}_{1}})\cap\text{dom}({{\Gamma}_{2}})=\varnothing
,
then
{\Gamma}_{1},{\Gamma}_{2};{\theta}\Entails{{P}\left[{\nicefrac{{{v}}}{{{m}}}}\right]}\TypedBy{\Delta}
.

Proof B.23
.

and

both hold as standard
[

,

,

,

]
.

(Well-typed Messages)
.

and

{\Gamma}\Entails{{v}:{T}}
,

and

,

{\Gamma};{\theta}\Entails{{qp}:{h}\cdot{l}b}\TypedBy{\Delta},{{qp}:\mathbf{\mathtt{M}};{{l}}\left\langle{{T}}\right\rangle},{b:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})}

:

Equation

, where we use the given assumptions as premise:

B.4.5.
Type-checking Message Handling

Lemma 8
.

Let
{\Delta},{{qp}:{{l}}\left\langle{{T}}\right\rangle;\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}}
be
well-formed
.
If
{\Gamma};{\theta}\Entails{{P}}\TypedBy{\Delta},{{qp}:{{l}}\left\langle{{T}}\right\rangle;\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}}
,
then
p\in\mathtt{NEQ}\left({{P}}\right)
.

Proof B.25
.

By induction on the typing derivation,
the key rules are
\left[{\mathtt{{VQue}}}\right]
are
\left[{\mathtt{{DQue}}}\right]
, which are structured such that:

{\Gamma};{\theta}\Entails{{qp}:{{{l}}{{v}}}\cdot{h}}\TypedBy{\Delta},\penalty\ {{qp}:{m};\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}}

where
{m}={{l}}\left\langle{{T}}\right\rangle
.
The thesis follows
Figure

3
, as
p\in\mathtt{NEQ}\left({{qp}:{{{l}}{{v}}}\cdot{h}}\right)
holds.

Lemma 9
.

Let
{\Delta}
be
well-formed
.
If
{\Gamma};{\theta}\Entails{{P}}\TypedBy{\Delta}

and
p\in\mathtt{NEQ}\left({{P}}\right)
,
then
\exists{\Delta}^{\prime}
such that
{\Delta}={\Delta}^{\prime},\penalty\ {{qp}:{{l}}\left\langle{{T}}\right\rangle;\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}}
.

Proof B.26
.

By the definition of
\mathtt{NEQ}\left({{P}}\right)
in
Figure

3
, it must be that

{P}\equiv{{qp}:{{l}}{{v}}\cdot{h}}\mid{Q}
,
and by
Note

B.18
of
3
, we obtain our thesis.

Lemma 10
.

Let
{\Delta}
be
well-formed
.
If
{\Gamma};{\theta}\Entails{{P}}\TypedBy{\Delta}

and
\mathtt{NEQ}\left({{P}}\right)=\varnothing
,
then
\mathtt{NEQ}\left({{\Delta}}\right)=\varnothing
.

Proof B.27
.

Since
\mathtt{NEQ}\left({{P}}\right)=\varnothing
, it follows that
\forall{qp}:{h}
such that
{P}\equiv{qp}:{h}\mid{Q}
, then
{h}=\varnothing
.
By
3
in
3
,
{\Delta}={\Delta}_{1},{\Delta}_{2}
and,

{\Gamma};{\theta}\Entails{qp}:{h}\TypedBy{\Delta}_{1}

and

{\Gamma};{\theta}\Entails{Q}\TypedBy{\Delta}_{2}
.
By
Note

B.17
in
3
, it follows that
{\Delta}_{1}={\Delta}^{\prime}_{1},{{qp}:\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}}
and
\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}=\varnothing
.
We obtain our thesis:
given that all message queues in
{P}
are empty, if
{P}
is
well-typed
against
{\Delta}
then, there exists corresponding queues in
{\Delta}
that are all also empty.

B.4.6.
Delayable Session Environments

Lemma 11
.

Let
{\Delta}
be
well-formed
.
If
{\Delta}
is
balanced
and

and
{\Delta}+{t}
is
well-formed
,
then
{\Delta}+{t}
is
balanced
.

Proof B.28
.

We show that
{\Delta}+{t}
satisfies all conditions of
Section

6
:

Case 1.

Since
{\Delta}
is
delayable
, by
Section

B.1.1
, all queues in the domain of
{\Delta}
are
empty
.
Therefore, the case where
{\Delta}={\Delta}^{\prime},{p:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {S}})},{{qp}:{m};\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}}
, conflicts with the hypothesis, and is not applicable.

Case 2.

If
{\Delta}={\Delta}^{\prime},{p:({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})},{{qp}:\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}},{q:({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2}})},{{pq}:\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}}
,
then
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\Compat({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})
.
Since
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})
and
({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2}})
are
well-formed
,
then by
15
and
17
,

({{\nu}_{1}+{t},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})
and
({{\nu}_{2}+{t},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2}})
are
well-formed
,
and
({{\nu}_{1}+{t},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}})\Compat({{\nu}_{2}+{t},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})
.
Therefore,

{\Delta}+{t}
is
balanced
.

Case 3.

If
{\Delta}={\Delta}^{\prime},{p:({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})},{{qp}:\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}},{q:({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2}})}
,
then
\exists\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}:({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}})\Compat({{\nu}_{2},}{\penalty\ {S}_{2},}{\penalty\ \mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{2}})
and the thesis follows
2
.

Lemma 12
.

If
{\Delta}
is
balanced
,
well-formed
and not
{t}
-reading,
then
{\Delta}
is
delayable
.

Proof B.29
.

If we suppose by contradiction that
{\Delta}
is
not

delayable
,
then by
Section

B.1.1
,
\exists{qp},{m},\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}
such that
{qp}\in\text{dom}({{\Delta}})
and
{\Delta}({qp})={m};\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}
and
\exists p\in\text{dom}({{\Delta}})
.
Since
{\Delta}
is
balanced
, for some
{\Delta}(p)=({{\nu}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}})

it follows

1
of
Section

6
:

{\Delta}={\Delta}^{\prime},{p:({{\nu}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}})},{{qp}:{m};\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}}\implies({{\nu}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{?{m}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime}{}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}})\quad\text{and}\quad{\Delta}^{\prime},{p:({{\nu}^{\prime}{}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}})},{{qp}:\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}}\in\text{Bal}

However, this contradicts with
{\Delta}
not
{t}
-reading:
by
Section

5.1
, there are no roles
q\in\text{dom}({{\Delta}})
such that
{\Delta}(q)=({{\nu}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}{}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}})
and
({{\nu}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}+{t}^{\prime},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}{}^{\prime}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{?{m}^{\prime}}}}$}}\,
, for all
{t}^{\prime}<{t}
.
Therefore,
{\Delta}
must be
delayable
.

Lemma 13
.

Let
{\Delta}
be

.

.

Case 1.

{P}=p^{{e}}\,{\MathOpPrcRecv\mathstrut}\begin{array}[t]{\{}{@{}l@{}}\}{{l}_{i}}{\left({{v}_{i}}\right)}:{P}_{i}\end{array}_{i\in I}\mid{qp}:{h}

of

of

,

\neg(\left\lvert J\right\rvert=\left\lvert I\right\rvert=1)
.

, then
\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{j}=?

and:

By

B.9
of

,

{\Delta}^{\prime}_{1}
is not
{e}
-reading, and
\forall{t}:{\nu}_{1}+{t}\models{\delta}_{j}\iff{t}\in{e}

of

,

({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ \begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}{\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{j}}\,{{l}_{j}}\left\langle{{T}_{j}}\right\rangle{\left({{{\delta}_{j}},{{\lambda}_{j}}}\right)}.{{S}_{j}}\end{array}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{?{{l}}_{j}\left\langle{{T}}_{j}\right\rangle}}}$}}\,({{\nu}_{1}\left[{{{\lambda}_{j}}\mapsto 0}\right],}{\penalty\ {S}_{j}})

By

,

.

By

B.17
of

,

{\Delta}_{2}={\Delta}^{\prime}_{2},{{qp}:\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}_{1}}

and since

It follows that
{\Delta}_{2}
is
delayable
, since there cannot be any other queues present in

is

is

.

Case 2.

If
{P}=p^{\diamond{n}}\,{\MathOpPrcRecv\mathstrut}\begin{array}[t]{\{}{@{}l@{}}\}{{l}_{i}}{\left({{v}_{i}}\right)}:{P}_{i}\end{array}_{i\in I}\penalty\ \mathtt{after}\penalty\ {{Q}}\mid{qp}:{h}

(B.1)

.

We only show the key cases of

Lemma 14
.

A simple induction on the typing derivation.

Lemma 15
.

{\Delta}

, as required.

B.5.
Reduction Steps

B.5.1.
Time-passing Steps

Lemma 16
.

Let
{\Delta}
be
well-formed
.

.

and

:

If rule
\left[{\mathtt{{VRecv}}}\right]
,

and following

6

Equation

5.6
:

{\Gamma};{\theta}\Entails{p^{{e}}\,{\MathOpPrcRecv\mathstrut}\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}{{l}}{\left({{v}}\right)}:{P}\end{array}}\TypedBy{\Delta}^{\prime},\penalty\ {p:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {?\mathstrut}{{l}}{\left\langle{{T}}\right\rangle}\left({\delta},{\lambda}\right).{{S}}})}\begin{array}[t]{@{}c@{}}{T}\penalty\ \text{\emph{base-type}}\quad{\Delta}^{\prime}\penalty\ \text{not\ ${e}$-reading}\quad{\forall{t}^{\prime}:{\nu}+{t}^{\prime}\models{\delta}\MathOpBimplies{t}^{\prime}\in{e}}\\[3.01385pt]
\forall{t}^{\prime}\in{e}:{\Gamma},{{v}:{T}};{\theta}+{t}^{\prime}\Entails{{P}}\TypedBy{\Delta}^{\prime}+{t}^{\prime},\penalty\ {p:({{\nu}+{t}^{\prime}\left[{{{\lambda}}\mapsto 0}\right],}{\penalty\ {S}})}\end{array}

where by

B.9
of
3
,
{\Delta}={\Delta}^{\prime},{p:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ {?\mathstrut}{{l}}{\left\langle{{T}}\right\rangle}\left({\delta},{\lambda}\right).{{S}}})}

is defined, by

, it must be that
{t}\in{e}
.

Coinciding with the first premise of rule

, it follows that

; additionally, by the second premise it follows that the time-step preserves

:

\forall{t}^{\prime}\in{e}:{\Gamma},{v}:{T};{\theta}+{t}^{\prime}\Entails{P}^{\prime}\TypedBy{\Delta}^{\prime}+{t}^{\prime},\penalty\ {p:({{\nu}+{t}^{\prime}\left[{{{\lambda}}\mapsto 0}\right],}{\penalty\ {S}})}

It remains to show that

is

.
By

{\Delta}+{t}
is
live
.
By

it is
well-formed
.

Case 2.

If rule
\left[{\mathtt{{DRecv}}}\right]
,
then we obtain our thesis analogously to
1
.

Case 3.

If rule
\left[{\mathtt{{Branch}}}\right]
,
then
{P}=p^{{e}}\,{\MathOpPrcRecv\mathstrut}\begin{array}[t]{\{}{@{}l@{}}\}{{l}_{i}}{\left({{v}_{i}}\right)}:{P}_{i}\end{array}_{i\in I}

{\Gamma};{\theta}\Entails p^{{e}}\,{\MathOpPrcRecv\mathstrut}\begin{array}[t]{\{}{@{}l@{}}\}{{l}_{i}}{\left({{v}_{i}}\right)}:{P}_{i}\end{array}_{i\in I}\TypedBy{\Delta}^{\prime},\penalty\ {p:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ \begin{array}[t]{\{}{@{}l@{}}\}{\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{j}}\,{{l}_{j}}\left\langle{{T}_{j}}\right\rangle{\left({{{\delta}_{j}},{{\lambda}_{j}}}\right)}.{{S}_{j}}\end{array}_{j\in J}})}\begin{array}[t]{@{}c@{}}\neg(\left\lvert J\right\rvert=\left\lvert I\right\rvert=1)\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}\forall j\in J:{\nu}\models{\delta}_{j}\implies\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{j}=?\penalty\ \land\penalty\ \exists i\in I:{l}_{i}={l}_{j}\penalty\ \land\penalty\ \mbox{}\\[3.01385pt]
\mbox{}\qquad{\Gamma};{\theta}\Entails{p^{{e}}\,{\MathOpPrcRecv\mathstrut}\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}{{l}_{i}}{\left({{v}_{i}}\right)}:{P}_{i}\end{array}}\TypedBy{\Delta}^{\prime},\penalty\ {p:({{\nu},}{\penalty\ \begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}{\mbox{\tiny{$\square$}}_{j}}\,{{l}_{j}}\left\langle{{T}_{j}}\right\rangle{\left({{{\delta}_{j}},{{\lambda}_{j}}}\right)}.{{S}_{j}}\end{array}})}\end{array}\end{array}

({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{?{m}}}}$}}\,({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1}})

and

{\Delta},{p:({{\nu}^{\prime}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}^{\prime}_{1}})},{{qp}:\mathbf{\mathtt{M}}^{\prime}_{1}}

is
balanced
.
Since
({{\nu}_{1},}{\penalty\ {S}_{1}})\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}[12.5pt]{${\mathrel{\to}^{\rule{0.0pt}{4.52083pt}{?{m}}}}$}}\,
, by
4
,
p\in\mathtt{Wait}\left({{P}}\right)
.
By
8
,
p\in\mathtt{NEQ}\left({{P}}\right)
.
However, since
\Phi_{t}\left({{P}}\right)
is defined, by
1
,
\mathtt{Wait}\left({{P}}\right)\cap\mathtt{NEQ}\left({{P}}\right)=\varnothing
, which is a contradiction.
Therefore, all queues must be empty and
{\Delta}+{t}
is
fully-balanced
.

!timeout(x>3,\penalty\ \varnothing).\mathtt{end}\end{array}\end{array}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{@{\quad}c@{}}\hskip 8.50012pt\lx@intercol\hfil\begin{array}[c]{@{}c@{}}\varnothing;\penalty\ {\theta}_{6.1}+0.1\Entails p\,{\MathOpPrcSend\mathstrut}\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}{timeout}\end{array}.{\texttt{0}}\TypedBy\penalty\ p:\begin{array}[t]{(}{@{}l@{\ }l@{}}){({\nu}_{6.1}[x\mapsto 3])+0.1},&\begin{array}[t]{\{}{@{}l@{}}\}?ping(x\leq 3,\penalty\ \{x\}).\mathtt{end},\\
!timeout(x>3,\penalty\ \varnothing).\mathtt{end}\end{array}\end{array}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{@{\quad}c@{}}\hskip 8.50012pt\lx@intercol\hfil\begin{array}[c]{@{}c@{}}\varnothing;\penalty\ {\theta}_{6.2}\Entails{\texttt{0}}\TypedBy\penalty\ p:\begin{array}[t]{(}{@{}l@{\ }l@{}}){{\nu}_{6.2}[x\mapsto 3.1]},&\mathtt{end}\end{array}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{@{\quad}c@{}}\hskip 8.50012pt\lx@intercol\hfil\begin{array}[c]{@{}c@{}}\varnothing;\penalty\ {\theta}_{6.2}\Entails{\texttt{0}}\TypedBy\penalty\ \varnothing\end{array}\hskip 8.50012pt\left[{\mathtt{{End}}}\right]\end{array}\end{array}\\
